It just feels like this heavy judgment has already been passed on me, and there's nothing I can do about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself constantly thinking about your father's decline and how you could have done things differently?\n\nPatient: Yes. I keep going over every single decision I made about his appointments. I just replay them in my head, along with every tense conversation I've had with my relatives about his care. I mean... I just end up feeling like I'm a completely selfish daughter. And it's not just at home, I feel like a useless employee at work, too. I just really feel like I deserve whatever happens to me now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to perform any specific rituals or behaviors to try and alleviate these feelings of guilt and judgment?\n\nPatient: No, I don't... I don't do anything like that. I don't have any specific habits or routines I do to try and make it go away. I just... I just sit with it, really. Like I said, it feels like the judgment has already been passed on me, so I don't try to fix it or make it better. I just live with the guilt.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. Most days I wake up with this... heavy, hollow feeling. It's really like the color has just gone out of everything. I can sit at my desk in HR, or just stare at the garden beds, and I feel like none of it reaches me anymore. So, yes, I would say I'm sad most of the time.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It's been going on for about a year and two months now. That's when my father's health really started to decline and his behavior changed... and I had to step in and take over his caregiving. That's when all of this started.\n\nDoctor: Do you find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I used to love gardening... being out in the yard, working with the plants. It was relaxing. But now, I just... I don't care about it anymore. I look out the window at the weeds growing, and I just feel nothing. I haven't touched my gardening tools in months. Nothing really brings me any joy or satisfaction these days.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in your interest in social activities or being around other people?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I still see people at work and talk to my coworkers in the office. I haven't really noticed a change in wanting to be around others, or avoiding anyone. I just go about my day with them like usual, even if I'm feeling empty inside. I wouldn't say I've lost interest in being around people.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your appetite or interest in food?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed any change there. I still eat my regular meals... you know, lunch at the office, dinner when I get home. My appetite seems pretty much the same as it's always been.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to find pleasurable, besides gardening?\n\nPatient: No, it's pretty much just the gardening that stands out. I mean, I guess I don't really do much else anyway. Work takes up a lot of my time in HR, and by the time I get home, I just... I don't really have hobbies besides the yard. So, no, I haven't noticed losing interest in anything else, mostly because there wasn't much else to begin with.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n11\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F33.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because... well, I just can't shake this absolute certainty that I'm being punished. It's about my father. I let him decline, and now I'm paying for it. My sister keeps trying to show me the doctor's notes, and she tells me I did everything right, but... it doesn't matter. It doesn't change anything. It just feels like this heavy judgment has already been passed on me, and there's nothing I can do about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself constantly thinking about your father's decline and how you could have done things differently?\n\nPatient: Yes. I keep going over every single decision I made about his appointments. I just replay them in my head, along with every tense conversation I've had with my relatives about his care. I mean... I just end up feeling like I'm a completely selfish daughter. And it's not just at home, I feel like a useless employee at work, too. I just really feel like I deserve whatever happens to me now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to perform any specific rituals or behaviors to try and alleviate these feelings of guilt and judgment?\n\nPatient: No, I don't... I don't do anything like that. I don't have any specific habits or routines I do to try and make it go away. I just... I just sit with it, really. Like I said, it feels like the judgment has already been passed on me, so I don't try to fix it or make it better. I just live with the guilt.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. Most days I wake up with this... heavy, hollow feeling. It's really like the color has just gone out of everything. I can sit at my desk in HR, or just stare at the garden beds, and I feel like none of it reaches me anymore. So, yes, I would say I'm sad most of the time.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It's been going on for about a year and two months now. That's when my father's health really started to decline and his behavior changed... and I had to step in and take over his caregiving. That's when all of this started.\n\nDoctor: Do you find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I used to love gardening... being out in the yard, working with the plants. It was relaxing. But now, I just... I don't care about it anymore. I look out the window at the weeds growing, and I just feel nothing. I haven't touched my gardening tools in months. Nothing really brings me any joy or satisfaction these days.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in your interest in social activities or being around other people?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. Especially on those mornings when I know there are going to be more calls from the care agency, and just... more problems waiting for me at work. It just feels like it would be a relief to not have to deal with it all.\n\nDoctor: Has your feeling of sadness and guilt been continuous over the past year and two months, or have you had periods where you felt somewhat better?\n\nPatient: It's been pretty continuous. Nothing really brings me any joy or satisfaction these days.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in your interest in social activities or being around other people?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I still see people at work and talk to my coworkers in the office. I haven't really noticed a change in wanting to be around others, or avoiding anyone. I just go about my day with them like usual, even if I'm feeling empty inside. I wouldn't say I've lost interest in being around people.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your appetite or interest in food?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed any change there. I still eat my regular meals... you know, lunch at the office, dinner when I get home. My appetite seems pretty much the same as it's always been.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to find pleasurable, besides gardening?\n\nPatient: No, it's pretty much just the gardening that stands out. I mean, I guess I don't really do much else anyway. Work takes up a lot of my time in HR, and by the time I get home, I just... I don't really have hobbies besides the yard. So, no, I haven't noticed losing interest in anything else, mostly because there wasn't much else to begin with.\n\nDoctor: Do you often think about death or dying?\n\nPatient: I don't have a plan to hurt myself or anything like that. But... I do catch myself thinking it would just be easier if I didn't wake up. Especially on those mornings when I know there are going to be more calls from the care agency, and just... more problems waiting for me at work. It just feels like it would be a relief to not have to deal with it all.\n\nDoctor: Has your feeling of sadness and guilt been continuous over the past year and two months, or have you had periods where you felt somewhat better?\n\nPatient: It's been pretty continuous. There haven't really been any periods where I felt better. This has all been going on for about a year and two months now... right around the time my father's health and behavior started changing. That's when I had to step into caregiving for him, and, well, it's just been this way ever since.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities, besides gardening, that you used to enjoy but no longer find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: I think you asked me that a minute ago... but no, like I said, it's really just the gardening. I don't really have any other hobbies. Between work and dealing with my dad, I didn't have much else going on anyway. That's when all of this started.\n\nDoctor: Do you find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I used to love gardening... being out in the yard, working with the plants. It was relaxing. But now, I just... I don't care about it anymore. I look out the window at the weeds growing, and I just feel nothing. I haven't touched my gardening tools in months. Nothing really brings me any joy or satisfaction these days.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in your interest in social activities or being around other people?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I still see people at work and talk to my coworkers in the office. I haven't really noticed a change in wanting to be around others, or avoiding anyone. I just go about my day with them like usual, even if I'm feeling empty inside. I wouldn't say I've lost interest in being around people.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your appetite or interest in food?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed any change there. I still eat my regular meals... you know, lunch at the office, dinner when I get home. My appetite seems pretty much the same as it's always been.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to find pleasurable, besides gardening?\n\nPatient: No, it's pretty much just the gardening that stands out. I mean, I guess I don't really do much else anyway. Work takes up a lot of my time in HR, and by the time I get home, I just... I don't really have hobbies besides the yard. So, no, I haven't noticed losing interest in anything else, mostly because there wasn't much else to begin with.\n\nDoctor: Do you often think about death or dying?\n\nPatient: I don't have a plan to hurt myself or anything like that. But... I do catch myself thinking it would just be easier if I didn't wake up. Especially on those mornings when I know there are going to be more calls from the care agency, and just... more problems waiting for me at work. It just feels like it would be a relief to not have to deal with it all.\n\nDoctor: Has your feeling of sadness and guilt been continuous over the past year and two months, or have you had periods where you felt somewhat better?\n\nPatient: It's been pretty continuous. There haven't really been any periods where I felt better. This has all been going on for about a year and two months now... right around the time my father's health and behavior started changing. That's when I had to step into caregiving for him, and, well, it's just been this way ever since.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities, besides gardening, that you used to enjoy but no longer find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: I think you asked me that a minute ago... but no, like I said, it's really just the gardening. I don't really have any other hobbies. Between work and dealing with my dad, I didn't have much else going on anyway. So there's nothing else I've lost interest in, just because there wasn't anything else to begin with.\n\nDoctor: Are there any other things you used to enjoy, besides gardening and work, that you no longer find enjoyable?\n\nPatient: I... I'm pretty sure you just asked me that. But no, my answer is still the same. It was really just the gardening. I didn't have the time or energy for any other hobbies before all this started anyway, so there's nothing else to list.\n\nDoctor: Are there any other things you used to look forward to that you no longer find enjoyable?\n\nPatient: Doctor, I feel like we are going in circles here... you literally just asked me this. No, there is nothing else. Just the gardening. Like I said, I really didn't have anything else I looked forward to besides that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came in because... well, I just can't shake this absolute certainty that I'm being punished. It's about my father. It just feels like this heavy judgment has already been passed on me, and there's nothing I can do about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself constantly thinking about your father's decline and how you could have done things differently?\n\nPatient: Yes. I keep going over every single decision I made about his appointments. I just replay them in my head, along with every tense conversation I've had with my relatives about his care. I mean... I just end up feeling like I'm a completely selfish daughter. And it's not just at home, I feel like a useless employee at work, too. I just really feel like I deserve whatever happens to me now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to perform any specific rituals or behaviors to try and alleviate these feelings of guilt and judgment?\n\nPatient: No, I don't... I don't do anything like that. I don't have any specific habits or routines I do to try and make it go away. I just... I just sit with it, really. Like I said, it feels like the judgment has already been passed on me, so I don't try to fix it or make it better. I just live with the guilt.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. Most days I wake up with this... heavy, hollow feeling. It's really like the color has just gone out of everything. I can sit at my desk in HR, or just stare at the garden beds, and I feel like none of it reaches me anymore. So, yes, I would say I'm sad most of the time.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It's been going on for about a year and two months now. That's when all of this started.\n\nDoctor: Do you find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I used to love gardening... being out in the yard, working with the plants. It was relaxing. But now, I just... I don't care about it anymore. I don't do anything like that. I don't have any specific habits or routines I do to try and make it go away. I just... I just sit with it, really. Like I said, it feels like the judgment has already been passed on me, so I don't try to fix it or make it better. I just live with the guilt.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. Most days I wake up with this... heavy, hollow feeling. It's really like the color has just gone out of everything. I can sit at my desk in HR, or just stare at the garden beds, and I feel like none of it reaches me anymore. So, yes, I would say I'm sad most of the time.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It's been going on for about a year and two months now. That's when my father's health really started to decline and his behavior changed... and I had to step in and take over his caregiving. That's when all of this started.\n\nDoctor: Do you find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I used to love gardening... being out in the yard, working with the plants. It was relaxing. But now, I just... I don't care about it anymore. I look out the window at the weeds growing, and I just feel nothing. I haven't touched my gardening tools in months. Nothing really brings me any joy or satisfaction these days.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in your interest in social activities or being around other people?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I still see people at work and talk to my coworkers in the office. I haven't really noticed a change in wanting to be around others, or avoiding anyone. I just go about my day with them like usual, even if I'm feeling empty inside. I wouldn't say I've lost interest in being around people.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your appetite or interest in food?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed any change there. I still eat my regular meals... you know, lunch at the office, dinner when I get home. I mean, I guess I don't really do much else anyway. Work takes up a lot of my time in HR, and by the time I get home, I just... I don't really have hobbies besides the yard. So, no, I haven't noticed losing interest in anything else, mostly because there wasn't much else to begin with.\n\nDoctor: Do you often think about death or dying?\n\nPatient: I don't have a plan to hurt myself or anything like that.
